    Since \( \psi \equiv \psi^{\prime} \) by assumption, we thus have \( \varphi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \)
    by \cref{logical-equivalence:replacing-logically-equivalent-conjuncts-or-disjuncts-yields-logically-equivalent-conjunctions-and-disjunctions}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{\textbf{Subcase 5}}: \( \psi = \lambda \) and \( \psi \folsubformula{\phi} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    We have \( \varphi = \phi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \) and
    \( \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} = \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi^{\prime} \).
    Since \( \phi \folsubformula \varphi \), it follows that \( \folcomplexityofformula{\phi} < n \), and thus the induction hypothesis applies to \( \psi \).
    Thus, \( \phi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \).
    By \cref{logical-equivalence:replacing-logically-equivalent-conjuncts-or-disjuncts-yields-logically-equivalent-conjunctions-and-disjunctions}, we infer that
    \( \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \equiv \phi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi^{\prime} \equiv \phi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \equiv \varphi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{\textbf{Subcase 6}}: \( \psi \folsubformula \phi \) and \( \psi \folnotsubformula \lambda \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Clearly \( \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} = \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \,\,{\land}\,\, \lambda \)
    and \( \phi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \).
    \( \varphi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \) follows readily from
    \cref{logical-equivalence:replacing-logically-equivalent-conjuncts-or-disjuncts-yields-logically-equivalent-conjunctions-and-disjunctions}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{\textbf{Subcase 7}}: \( \psi \folnotsubformula \phi \) and \( \psi \folsubformula \lambda \).
    
    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Same as previous subcase.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{\textbf{Subcase 8}}: \( \psi \folsubformula \phi \) and \( \psi \folsubformula \lambda \).
    Replacing \( \psi \) with \( \psi^{\prime} \) in \( \varphi \) requires both \( \phi \) and \( \lambda \) to be replaced.
    More specifically, \( \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} =
    \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \,\,{\land}\,\,
    \folsubformulasubstitution{\lambda}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \).
    Since \( \phi \) and \( \lambda \) are both subformulas of \( \varphi \), it follows that the induction hypothesis applies
    to the both of them.
    Thus, \( \varphi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    It has been demonstrated that \( \varphi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \) holds for all eight possibilities.
    Since \( \varphi \), \( \psi \), and \( \psi^{\prime} \) are arbitrary, we conclude that \( P(n) \) holds.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \varphi \coloneq \phi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \lambda \) for \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas \( \phi \) and \( \lambda \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Exactly the same as the previous case.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \varphi \coloneq \forall x \, \phi \) for some \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \phi \) and some \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable \( x \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( \psi \) be any subformula of \( \varphi \).
    There are two possibilities to consider: \( \psi = \phi \) or \( \psi \folsubformula \phi \).
    If \( \psi = \phi \), then clearly \( \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} = \forall x \, \psi^{\prime} \) and
    \( \varphi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \) is merely a consequence of
    \cref{logical-equivalence:replacing-logically-equivalent-formulas-in-quantified-formulas-yields-logically-equivalent-formulas}.
    If \( \psi \folsubformula \phi \), then \( \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} =
    \forall x \, \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \).
    But \( \phi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), so the induction hypothesis applies to it;
    i.e., \( \phi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\phi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \).
    Once again, we arrive at \( \varphi \equiv \folsubformulasubstitution{\varphi}{\psi}{\psi^{\prime}} \)
    by \cref{logical-equivalence:replacing-logically-equivalent-formulas-in-quantified-formulas-yields-logically-equivalent-formulas}.
    Since \( \varphi \), \( \psi \), and \( \psi^{\prime} \) are arbitrary, it follows that \( P(n) \) holds.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \varphi \coloneq \exists x \, \phi \) for some \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \phi \) and some \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable \( x \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Same argument as the previous case.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Thus, \( P(n) \) holds in all possible cases. Since \( n \in \naturalset \), the theorem follows immediately from strong induction.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced}
    If \( t \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-term, \( x \) and \( y \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that \( x \in \folvars{t} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{t} \),
    and \( t_{\ast} \) is the term obtained after all occurrences of \( x \) in \( t \) has been replaced with \( y \),
    then the following are true for any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure \( \mathfrak{A} \) and any variable assignment function \( \mathfrak{a} \):
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} =
                \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
        \item \( \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} =
                \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }\)
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    We proceed by induction on the complexity of \( \mathcal{L} \)-terms. Let \( P(n) \) be the following statement about an arbitrary natural number \( n \):
    for all \( \mathcal{L} \)-term \( t \) such that \( \folcomplexityofterm{t} = n \), if \( x \) and \( y \) are any two \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that
    \( x \in \folvars{t} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{t} \) and \( t_{\ast} \) denotes the \( \mathcal{L} \)-term obtained after every occurrence of \( x \)
    in \( t \) has been replaced with \( y \),
    then \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \) and
    \( \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) for any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure \( \mathfrak{A} \) and any variable assignment function \( \mathfrak{a} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Next, we state the following hypothesis for an arbitrary \( n \in \naturalset \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{\scshape Induction Hypothesis}: \( P(k) \) holds for all \( k < n \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( t \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-term such that \( \folcomplexityofterm{t} = n \).
    Let \( x \) and \( y \) denote any two \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that \( x \in \folvars{t} \)
    and \( y \notin \folvars{t} \). Moreover, let \( t_{\ast} \) denote the \( \mathcal{L} \)-term obtained
    after every occurrence of \( x \) in \( t \) has been replaced with \( y \).
    We must show that under these conditions, \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \) and \( \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    for any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure \( \mathfrak{A} \) and any variable assignment function \( \mathfrak{a} \).
    Since \( n \in \naturalset \), there are two cases to consider.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( n = 0 \).
    By \cref{cor:first-order-language-terms-with-zero-complexity-is-either-a-variable-or-0-ary-function-symbol}, necessarily
    \( t \coloneq x \) and thus \( t_{\ast} \coloneq y \).
    Note that \( t \) cannot consist of a 0-ary function symbol because that would imply \( x \notin \folvars{t} \), which contradicts our initial assumption.
    Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{b} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-interpretation-of-terms}, we have
    \begin{gather*}
        \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{b}}
        = \mathfrak{b}(x)
        = \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{\mathfrak{b}(x)} }\\
        \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{b}}
        = \mathfrak{b}(y)
        = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{\mathfrak{b}(y)} }
    \end{gather*}

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( n \neq 0 \). By \cref{cor:first-order-language-terms-with-non-zero-complexity-must-have-a-parent-function-symbol}, it follows that
    \( t \coloneq F^{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(\tau) \) for some \( i \in \dom F^{\mathcal{L}} \) where \( \tau \) is a tuple of \( \mathcal{L} \)-terms such that 
    \( \dom \tau = \Phi^{\mathcal{L}}(i) \). In this case, \( t_{\ast} \coloneq F^{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(\sigma) \) where \( \dom \sigma = \dom \tau \) and
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:define-sigma-tuple-of-terms-with-x-replaced-with-y}
        \sigma_{j} =
        \begin{cases}
            \ \tau_{j} \ &\text{if} \ x \notin \folvars{\tau_{j}}\\
            \mleft( \tau_{j} \mright)_{\ast} \ &\text{if} \  x \in \folvars{\tau_{j}}
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    for all \( j \in \dom \tau \).
    Let \( \mathfrak{C} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{c} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{C} \).
    Let \( m \in \dom \tau \) be arbitrary.
    By the \aref{rule-of-inference:law-of-excluded-middle}{Law of Excluded Middle}, either \( x \in \folvars{\tau_{m}} \) or \( x \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \) is true.
    If \( x \in \folvars{\tau_{m}} \), then \( \sigma_{m} = \mleft( \tau_{m} \mright)_{\ast} \) by \eqref{eq:define-sigma-tuple-of-terms-with-x-replaced-with-y}.
    But since \( \tau_{m} \) is a subterm of \( t \), then necessarily \( \folcomplexityofterm{\tau_{m}} < n \).
    Thus, the induction hypothesis guarantees that \( \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} =
    \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{\mathfrak{c}(x)} } \)
    and \( \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{\mathfrak{c}(y)} } =
    \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}}\).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    On the other hand, if \( x \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \), then \( \sigma_{m} \) and \( \tau_{m} \) are the same \( \mathcal{L} \)-term by
    \eqref{eq:define-sigma-tuple-of-terms-with-x-replaced-with-y}. Furthermore, since \( x \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \),
    it follows that
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{\folvars{\tau_{m}}} =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{\mathfrak{c}(x)} }{\folvars{\tau_{m}}} =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{\mathfrak{c}(y)} }{\folvars{\sigma_{m}}} =
        \restrictedfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{\folvars{\sigma_{m}}}
    \end{equation*}
    Thus, we conclude from \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-for-all-variable-symbols-in-a-term}
    that
    \begin{gather*}
        \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} =
        \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{ \mathfrak{c}(x) } } =
        \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{ \mathfrak{c}(x) } }\\
        \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} =
        \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{\mathfrak{c}(y)} } =
        \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{\mathfrak{c}(y)} }
    \end{gather*}
    Since \( m \in \dom \tau \) is arbitrary, it follows that
    \begin{gather}
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\tau_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} }{ j \in \dom \tau } =
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{\mathfrak{c}(x)} } }{ j \in \dom \tau }
        \phantomsection \label{eq:interpreted-tau-and-sigma-are-equal-1}\\
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} }{ j \in \dom \sigma } =
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\tau_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{\mathfrak{c}(y)} } }{ j \in \dom \tau }
        \phantomsection \label{eq:interpreted-tau-and-sigma-are-equal-2}
    \end{gather}
    Since \( F^{\mathfrak{C}}_{i} \) is a function, \cref{dfn:first-order-language-interpretation-of-terms}, \eqref{eq:interpreted-tau-and-sigma-are-equal-1}
    and \eqref{eq:interpreted-tau-and-sigma-are-equal-2} imply that
    \begin{gather*}
        \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} =
        F^{\mathfrak{C}}_{i} \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\tau_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} }{ j \in \dom \tau } =
        F^{\mathfrak{C}}_{i} \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{\mathfrak{c}(x)} } }{ j \in \dom \tau } =
        \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{ \mathfrak{c}(x) } }\\
        \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} =
        F^{\mathfrak{C}}_{i} \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{c}} }{ j \in \dom \tau } =
        F^{\mathfrak{C}}_{i} \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\tau_{j}}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{\mathfrak{c}(y)} } }{ j \in \dom \tau } =
        \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{C}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{ \mathfrak{c}(y) } }
    \end{gather*}
    Clearly, the induction hypothesis implies \( P(n) \) for any arbitrary \( n \in \naturalset \).
    Therefore, by the principle of strong induction, we conclude that \( P(n) \) is true for all \( n \in \naturalset \).
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{thm:satisfaction-of-formulas-with-replaced-free-variables}
    If \( \varphi \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula and \( x \) and \( y \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that
    \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{\varphi} \), then the following statements are true
    for any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure \( \mathfrak{A} \) and any variable assignment function \( \mathfrak{a} \):
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
            iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } }\)
        \item \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
            iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{ \mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    We proceed by induction on the complexity of formulas. Let \( P(n) \) be the following statement about any natural number \( n \):
    for all \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \varphi \) such that \( \folcomplexityofformula{\varphi} = n \), if \( x \) and \( y \)
    are any two \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \),
    then \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } }\) and
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) iff
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \varphi }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    for any structure \( \mathfrak{A} \) and any variable assignment function \( \mathfrak{a} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Next, we state the following hypothesis for an arbitrary \( n \in \naturalset \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{\scshape Induction Hypothesis}: \( P(k) \) is true for all \( k < n \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( \varphi \) denote any \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula such that \( \folcomplexityofformula{\varphi} = n \).
    Let \( x \) and \( y \) denote any two \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \).
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{a} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Since \( n \in \naturalset \), either \( n = 0 \) or \( n \neq 0 \).
    We must show that 
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } }\) and
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) iff
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \varphi }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    are true in both cases.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( n = 0 \). Clearly, \aref{cor:first-order-language-only-atoms-can-have-zero-complexity}{\( \varphi \) is an atom of \( \mathcal{L} \)}.
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-atom}, \( \varphi \) either has the form \( s = t \) where \( s \) and \( t \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-terms
    or \( \varphi \coloneq P^{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(\tau) \) for some \( i \in \dom P^{\mathcal{L}} \) where \( \tau \) is a tuple of \( \mathcal{L} \)-terms such that
    \( \dom \tau = \Pi^{\mathcal{L}}(i) \).
    If \( \varphi \) has the first form, then \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq s_{\diamond} = t_{\diamond} \) where \( s_{\diamond} \) and \( t_{\diamond} \)
    are defined as follows:
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:define-s-star-and-t-star-where-x-is-replaced-with-y}
        s_{\diamond} =
        \begin{cases}
            s \ &\text{if} \ x \notin \folvars{s} \\
            s_{\ast} \ &\text{if} \ x \in \folvars{s}
        \end{cases}
        \qquad
        t_{\diamond} =
        \begin{cases}
            t \ &\text{if} \ x \notin \folvars{t} \\
            t_{\ast} \ &\text{if} \ x \in \folvars{t}
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    Note that \( s_{\ast} \) and \( t_{\ast} \) are the \( \mathcal{L} \)-terms obtained after every occurrence (if any) of \( x \) in \( s \) and \( t \)
    respectively has been replaced with \( y \).
    Since \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \), \( x \) must either occur in \( s \) or \( t \), or in both \( s \) and \( t \).
    Therefore, there are three scenarios to consider.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 1}: \( x \in \folvars{s} \) and \( x \notin \folvars{t} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    By \eqref{eq:define-s-star-and-t-star-where-x-is-replaced-with-y}, it follows that \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq s_{\ast} = t \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t}
        \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}}
    \end{equation}
    Since \( x \in \folvars{s} \), we have by \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced}
    \( \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} =
    \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \).
    Moreover, since \( y \notin \folvars{t} \), it follows from
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-for-all-variable-symbols-in-a-term} that
    \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} =
    \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \).
    Combining these inferences with \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t} yields
    \begin{equation}
        \phantomsection \label{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t}
        \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } =
        \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } } \)
    by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}. In other words,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}}
        \ \text{implies}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation}
    To prove the converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t} is true.
    Since \( x \in \folvars{s} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{t} \), we can similarly infer \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t}
    from \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-for-all-variable-symbols-in-a-term,thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced}, which establishes the converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    We now proceed to prove that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}}
        \ \text{implies}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
    \end{equation}
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true;
    that is,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t-2}
        \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}}
    \end{equation}
    Given that \( x \in \folvars{s} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{s} \), it follows from
    \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced} that
    \( \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \). Furthermore, we also have \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) by \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-for-all-variable-symbols-in-a-term} because \( x \notin \folvars{t} \). Applying these facts to \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t-2} yields
    \begin{equation}
        \phantomsection \label{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t-2}
        \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
        =
        \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
    \end{equation}
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, we can easily conclude \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove the converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) is true;
    that is, \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t-2} holds.
    Again, we can infer \( \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) and \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) from
    \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced,thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-for-all-variable-symbols-in-a-term}.
    Substituting these equalities into \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t-2} yields \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t-2}, which establishes
    the converse.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 2}: \( x \notin \folvars{s} \) and \( x \in \folvars{t} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Same argument as the previous scenario but with the roles of \( s \) and \( t \) interchanged.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 3}: \( x \in \folvars{s} \) and \( x \in \folvars{t} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    By \eqref{eq:define-s-star-and-t-star-where-x-is-replaced-with-y}, we have \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq s_{\ast} = t_{\ast} \).
    Now suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true;
    that is, \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t} holds.
    Since neither \( x \) nor \( y \) occur in \( s \) and \( t \),
    \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced} applies to both \( s \) and \( t \), and we have
    \( \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \) and
    \( \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \).
    At this point, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }\)
    follows readily from \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-equals-interpreted-t} and
    \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} is established.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Conversely, assume that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }\) is true.
    Then \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) also follows from
    \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced,dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, thereby
    establishing the converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Next, we prove that \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication} and its converse are also true in this scenario.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true.
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t-star}
        \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}}
    \end{equation}
    Neither \( x \) nor \( y \) occur in \( s \) and \( t \), so
    \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced} applies to \( s \) and \( t \) to yield
    \( \folterminterpretation{s_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{s}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) and  \( \folterminterpretation{t_{\ast}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} = \folterminterpretation{t}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \). Substituting these equalities into \eqref{eq:interpreted-s-star-equals-interpreted-t-star} establishes \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication} can be proven the same way by supposing
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) to be true
    and then invoking \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced} to conclude
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    If \( \varphi \) takes the second form, then \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq P^{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(\sigma) \)
    where \( \sigma \) is a tuple of \( \mathcal{L} \)-terms such that \( \dom \sigma = \dom \tau \) and
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:define-sigma-tuple-of-L-terms}
        \sigma_{j} =
        \begin{cases}
           \ \tau_{j} \ &\text{if} \ x \notin \folvars{\tau_{j}}\\
            \mleft( \tau_{j} \mright)_{\ast} \ &\text{if} \ x \in \folvars{\tau_{j}}
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    for all \( j \in \dom \tau \).
    Note that \( \mleft( \tau_{j} \mright)_{\ast} \) denotes the \( \mathcal{L} \)-term obtained after every occurrence of \( x \) in \( \tau_{j} \) has been
    replaced with \( y \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( m \in \dom \tau \) be arbitrary. Clearly, either \( x \in \folvars{\tau_{m}} \) or \( x \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \).
    If \( x \in \folvars{\tau_{m}} \), then \( \sigma_{m} = \mleft( \tau_{m} \mright)_{\ast} \) by \eqref{eq:define-sigma-tuple-of-L-terms}.
    By \cref{thm:interpretation-of-terms-with-variable-symbols-replaced}, it follows that
    \begin{gather}
        \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} =
        \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
        \phantomsection \label{eq:interpreted-tau-j-equals-interpreted-sigma-j} \\
        \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{m}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} =
        \folterminterpretation{\tau_{m}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
        \phantomsection \label{eq:interpreted-sigma-j-equals-interpreted-tau-j}
    \end{gather}
    On the other hand, if \( x \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \), then \( \tau_{m} \) and \( \sigma_{m} \) are the same \( \mathcal{L} \)-term.
    Since \( x \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{\tau_{m}} \), it follows that
    \begin{gather*}
        \restrictedfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{\folvars{\tau_{m}}} = 
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{\folvars{\tau_{m}}}\\
        \restrictedfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{ \folvars{\tau_{m}} } =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{\folvars{\tau_{m}}}
    \end{gather*}
    Since \( \tau_{m} \) and \( \sigma_{m} \) are the same term,
    \eqref{eq:interpreted-tau-j-equals-interpreted-sigma-j}
    and \eqref{eq:interpreted-sigma-j-equals-interpreted-tau-j} are also true in this case due to
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-for-all-variable-symbols-in-a-term}.
    Given that \( m \in \dom \tau \) is arbitrary, we can infer that
    \begin{gather*}
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\tau_{j}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} }{j \in \dom \tau} =
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{j}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } }}{j \in \dom \tau}\\
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\sigma_{j}}{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{a}} }{ j \in \dom \tau } =
        \sequencebig{ \folterminterpretation{\tau_{j}}{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{ \mathfrak{a}(y) } } }{j \in \dom \tau}
    \end{gather*}
    It should now be obvious from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication},
    \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, and their respective converses are all true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( n \neq 0 \). In this case, \( \varphi \) must have one of the forms stated in \cref{cor:first-order-language-every-formula-with-non-zero-complexity-can-be-decomposed}.
    If \( \varphi \coloneq \mleft( \psi \mright) \) or \( \varphi \coloneq \mleft[ \,\psi\, \mright] \) for some \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \psi \), then clearly
    \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \mleft( \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \mright) \) and
    \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \mleft[ \,\folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y}\, \mright] \)
    respectively because the symbol \( x \) can only occur in \( \psi \).
    Note that \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), which means that \( \folcomplexityofformula{\psi} < n \).
    In other words, the induction hypothesis applies to \( \psi \) and we have
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) iff
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    and
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) iff
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{ \mathfrak{A} }{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, we have
    \begin{gather*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \ \text{iff} \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \ \text{iff}
        \  \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
        \ \text{iff}
        \  \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }\\
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \ \text{iff}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \ \text{iff}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{ \mathfrak{A} }{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \ \text{iff}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{ \mathfrak{A} }{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}}
    \end{gather*}

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Similarly, if \( \varphi \coloneq \lnot \, \psi \), then \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \lnot \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \)
    because the variable symbol \( x \) can only occur in \( \psi \).
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \),
    we can infer \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}, \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, and their respective converses
    from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} and the induction hypothesis as follows:
    \begin{gather*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \ \text{iff} \ \folwffnonsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}}
        \ \text{iff} \ \folwffnonsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
        \ \text{iff} \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }\\
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}}
        \ \text{iff} \ \folwffnonsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}}
        \ \text{iff} \ \folwffnonsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
        \ \text{iff} \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
    \end{gather*}
    If \( \varphi \coloneq \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \phi \) for \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas \( \psi \) and \( \phi \),
    then clearly \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \,\,{\land}\,\, \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} \).
    Since \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \), there are three scenarios to consider.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 1}: \( x \in \folfreevars{\psi} \) and \( x \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \); that is, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) are both true.
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), \( x \in \folfreevars{\psi} \), and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    by the induction hypothesis.
    Furthermore, since \( y \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \), it follows from
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \).
    But \( \phi \) and \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} \) are identical formulas since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \).
    Therefore, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }\)
    is true.
    In other words, the supposition \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) implies both
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    and
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }\),
    thus establishing \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} can be proven in a similar fashion by
    supposing \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    to be true and then invoking
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}
    and the induction hypothesis to conclude \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \);
    i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \mathfrak{a}} \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{ \mathfrak{a} } \) are both true.
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), \( x \in \folfreevars{\psi} \), and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \),
    the induction hypothesis applies to \( \psi \) and we have 
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \).
    Observe that \( \phi \) and \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} \) denote identical formulas since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \).
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) must also hold.
    But \( \mathfrak{a} \) and \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} \) agree everywhere in \( \folfreevars{\phi} \)
    because \( x \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \),
    so we conclude from \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) and
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}
    that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) must be true.
    In other words,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    follows from the supposition
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    as claimed in \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication} can be proven by simply arguing backwards starting
    from the suppostion that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 2:} \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \) and \( x \in \folfreevars{\phi} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Same proof as the previous scenario only with the roles of \( \psi \) and \( \phi \) interchanged.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 3:} \( x \in \folfreevars{\psi} \) and \( x \in \folfreevars{\phi} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true. Thus, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) both hold.
    Since \( x \) and \( y \) do not occur in \( \varphi \), they cannot possibly occur in \( \psi \) and \( \phi \).
    This means that the induction hypothesis can be applied to \( \psi \) and \( \phi \) and we have
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
        \ \ \text{and}
        \ \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation*}
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \),
    which establishes \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} can be proven the same way using the induction hypothesis.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To establish \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true;
    i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) are both true.
    Once again, it follows from the induction hypothesis that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
        \ \ \text{and}
        \ \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }
    \end{equation*}
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, we have
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    as required. The converse can be proven the same way.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    If \( \varphi \) has the form \( \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \phi \), then \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \,\,{\lor}\,\, \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} \).
    Since \( \varphi \) consists of two subformulas \( \psi \) and \( \phi \), we have to prove that \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}, \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, and their respective converses hold for the same three scenarios above.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    \underline{Scenario 1}: \( x \in \folfreevars{\psi} \) and \( x \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \).

    \vspace{0.1cm}
    \noindent
    Assume that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds; that is, either
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) or \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true.
    If \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \), then clearly
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-replaced-x-with-y-is-satisfied-by-modifiedassignmentfunction-y-changed-to-x}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation}
    by the induction hypothesis. But \eqref{eq:psi-replaced-x-with-y-is-satisfied-by-modifiedassignmentfunction-y-changed-to-x}
    clearly implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}. On the other hand, if \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true,
    then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:phi-replaced-x-with-y-is-satisfied-by-modifiedassignmentfunction-y-changed-to-x}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation}
    must also be true by \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}
    since \( y \notin \folfreevars{\phi} \) and \( \phi = \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} \).
    But if \eqref{eq:phi-replaced-x-with-y-is-satisfied-by-modifiedassignmentfunction-y-changed-to-x} holds, then
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    must also hold by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.
    Since
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    is implied by both disjuncts, we infer by \aref{rule-of-inference:disjunction-elimination}{Disjunction Elimination} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    is true. Once again, this establishes \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} can be proven using the same argument, only in reverse.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Next, we establish the truth of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true;
    that is, either \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    or \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true.
    Recall that both \( \psi \) and \( \phi \) are subformulas of \( \varphi \), which means that the induction hypothesis applies
    to both of them. Thus, either possibility implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \), which proves
    \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication} can be proven just as easily.
    Begin with the supposition that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) and then
    deduce from the induction hypothesis that either \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) or
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\phi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) must be true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Now we consider the case where \( \varphi \) is a universally quantified formula; i.e., \( \varphi \coloneq \forall z \, \psi \) for some \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \psi \)
    and some \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable \( z \). Clearly \( x \) can only occur in \( \psi \), so we have
    \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \forall z \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \).
    Note also that \( z \neq x \) because \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( z \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \).
    Similarly, \( z \neq y \) because \( y \notin \folvars{\varphi} \) and \( z \in \folvars{\varphi} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    We now proceed to establish the truth of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} and its converse.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true; that is,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-e-for-all-e}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. It follows immediately from \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-e-for-all-e} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), it follows from the induction hypothesis that
    \begin{equation}
        \phantomsection \label{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y}}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } }
    \end{equation}
    But \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) = \mathfrak{a}(x) \) because \( x \neq z \).
    Since \( y \) and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables, we have by
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}
    \begin{equation}
        \phantomsection \label{eq:rearranging-the-order-of-modification-for-modified-a}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) }
     = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation}
    It follows immediately from \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) is arbitrary and \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \forall z \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \),
    we conclude from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } } \)
    is true, which suffices to establish \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    The converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} can be proven with a similar argument.
    Assume the truth of
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } } \);
    i.e.,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-y-becomes-ax}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e} } \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary.
    It follows from \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-y-becomes-ax} that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0-2}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation}
    Again, since \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are all distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables, \eqref{eq:rearranging-the-order-of-modification-for-modified-a} holds and we obtain
    \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0}, from which we can infer (by the induction hypothesis) that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} } \).
    Given that \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) is arbitrary, we conclude that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Thus, the converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} is true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:varphi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-a}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. It follows immediately from \eqref{eq:varphi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-a} that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-z-mapsto-e0}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation}
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), we deduce from the induction hypothesis
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } }
    \end{equation}
    Since \( y \) and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables, it follows that \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) = \mathfrak{a}(y) \).
    Thus,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:a-modified-z-e0-modified-y-ay}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)}
        = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation}
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    We then infer from the combination of \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay} and \eqref{eq:a-modified-z-e0-modified-y-ay} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( e_{0} \) represents an arbitrary element of \( \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \), we conclude that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \), which proves \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove the converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, we reason as follows.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) holds; that is,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:varphi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \psi }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} 
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. By \eqref{eq:varphi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay}, it follows that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay-modified-z-mapsto-e0}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation}
    Once again, we note the fact that \( y \) and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables. Thus, \eqref{eq:a-modified-z-e0-modified-y-ay} applies
    and \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay-modified-z-mapsto-e0} implies \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay} and subsequently
    \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-z-mapsto-e0} by the induction hypothesis.
    Since \( e_{0} \) is arbitrary, it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true, thus
    establishing the converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Finally, we consider the case where \( \varphi \) is an existentially quantified formula; i.e., \( \varphi \coloneq \exists z \, \psi \) for some \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula
    \( \psi \) and \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable \( z \) such that \( z \neq x \) and \( z \neq y \).
    Clearly, every free occurrence of \( x \) in \( \varphi \) is also a free occurence in \( \psi \).
    Therefore, \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \exists z \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \).
    Again, we must show that \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}, \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication} and their respective converses are true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \). Thus, there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-for-some-e0}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \psi }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
        \ \ \text{for some} \  e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), applying the induction hypothesis to \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-for-some-e0} once again
    yields \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0}.
    However,
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) }
     = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    due to \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}
    and the fact that \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables.
    From \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0}, we can now infer
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}} }      
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \exists z \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \), we conclude by
    \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation*}
    This establishes \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} can be proven using a similar reasoning, only in reverse.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    is true; that is, there exists some \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables, we have by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } }
    \end{equation*}
    By the induction hypothesis, we then have
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    The truth of \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is merely a consequence of
    \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    is true. Thus, there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), we conclude from the induction hypothesis that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } }
    \end{equation*}
    But \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) = \mathfrak{a}(y) \) because \( z \neq y \).
    Additionally, the fact that \( x \neq z \) means that \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct} is applicable and we have
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } =
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    In other words, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }  \) is true and thus \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) as required.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove the converse, we reason like so.
    Suppose that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) is true; that is,
    there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \(
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }
    \) is true.
    Since \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are distinct, it follows from
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } =
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    Thus, \(\folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }\) implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } } \), which in turn implies
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    by the induction hypothesis.
    The truth of \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \mathfrak{a} } \) follows readily from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    It has been shown that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } }\)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{ \mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    hold for any \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \varphi \) with \( \folcomplexityofformula{\varphi} = n \) as long as the induction hypothesis is true.
    In other words, for any arbitrary \( n \in \naturalset \), the induction hypothesis implies \( P(n) \).
    The theorem follows readily from strong induction.
\end{proof}

\newpage
\section{Applications}

\noindent
We now have enough theoretical foundation to start proving many of the familiar results from logic.
\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:the-order-of-quantifiers-of-the-same-type-does-not-matter}
    Let \( \varphi \) denote an \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula. Let \( x \) and \( y \) denote \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables.
    Then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \forall x \, \forall y \, \varphi \equiv \forall y \, \forall x \, \varphi \) \phantomsection \label{enum:swapping-universal-quantifiers}
        \item \( \exists x \, \exists y \, \varphi \equiv \exists y \, \exists x \, \varphi \) \phantomsection \label{enum:swapping-existential-quantifiers}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \psi \coloneq \forall y \, \varphi \) and \( \phi \coloneq \forall x \, \varphi \).
    To prove \ref{enum:swapping-universal-quantifiers}, we must demonstrate that \( \forall x \, \psi \equiv \forall y \, \phi \).
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{a} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \)
    under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \); that is,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:forall-x-psi-is-satisfied-with-a}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. It follows immediately from \eqref{eq:forall-x-psi-is-satisfied-with-a} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    Let \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. Since
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \)
    and \( \psi \coloneq \forall y \, \varphi \),
    it follows from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \).
    We now consider the only two possibilities that can arise under these circumstances: either \( x \) and \( y \) are the same \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable (\( x = y \))
    or they are distinct (\( x \neq y \)).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    If \( x = y \), then \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{1}} = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} \) by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-last-modification-will-take-effect-if-the-same-variable-symbol-is-modified-for-a-variable-assignment-function}.
    If \( x \neq y \),
    then \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}}
    = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{1}} }{x}{e_{0}} \) by
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    For either case, it is clear that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \)
    implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \).
    Given that \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) is arbitrary,
    it follows from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright) }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} } \);
    i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} } \). But \( e_{0} \) is also arbitrary,
    so necessarily \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall y \, \phi \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    In other words,
    \begin{equation*}
        \forall x \, \psi \models \forall y \, \phi
    \end{equation*}
    For the converse, let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be an \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{b} \) be any variable assignment function
    on \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \). Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall y \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    Thus,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:forall-y-phi-is-satisfied-with-b}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) be arbitrary. Therefore, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} } \)
    by \eqref{eq:forall-y-phi-is-satisfied-with-b}.
    Let \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) be another arbitrary element of \( \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \).
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} } \) and
    \( \phi \coloneq \forall x \, \varphi \), it follows from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \).
    Again, we must consider two separate cases: \( x = y \) or \( x \neq y \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    If \( x = y \), then \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} \)
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-last-modification-will-take-effect-if-the-same-variable-symbol-is-modified-for-a-variable-assignment-function}.
    If \( x \neq y \), then
    \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} \) is also true
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    Clearly,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \)
    implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \)
    for either case.
    Since \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) is arbitrary, we infer from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    But \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) is also arbitrary, so we conclude
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \mleft( \forall x \, \psi \mright) }{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        \forall y \, \phi \models \forall x \, \psi
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \forall x \, \psi \models \forall y \, \phi \) and \( \forall y \, \phi \models \forall x \, \psi \) are both true,
    it follows that \( \forall x \, \psi \equiv \forall y \, \phi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
